# Database Management Systems: question papers in full

197 unique papers · 2005-06 to 2025-26 · Information Technology, Computer Engineering, Computer Applications (MCA), Data Science, Artificial Intelligence, Computer Science and Business Systems, Cyber Security, AI and Data Science, AI and Machine Learning, Computer Science, Common (all branches)

Each paper: year, exam, semester, departments and programmes, then its full text as extracted. Scanned papers were read by OCR, so equations and symbols may contain recognition errors. Structured data: [`questions.jsonl`](questions.jsonl) (one record per question) and [`papers.jsonl`](papers.jsonl) (one record per paper). Index of files: [`README.md`](README.md).

SO - 2;
BL-2   Problem Statement:
--- page 2 ---
A university offers multiple programs (e.g., B.Tech IT, B.Tech CS). Each
program has a unique Program_ID, name, and duration.
Each program consists of several courses; each course has a Course_Code,
Course_Name, and credits. A course may be taught by one or more
instructors; each instructor has an Instructor_ID, name, designation, and
email.
Students enroll in one program and are identified by Student_ID, name,
email, and year_of_admission.
Students can register for multiple courses each semester, and for each
registration, the system stores semester, academic_year, and grade.
CO- 2 ;   b   Consider following movie database:   [10]
SO - 2;
BL- 2   users (user_id, full_name, city, contact_no)
restaurants (restaurant_id, name, address, price level, is active)
dishes (dish_id, restaurant, name, price, is_veg, is spicy)
ratings (user_id, dish_id, rating, rated_at, feedback)
Write SQL statements to perform the following operations.
i.   Add a new column “email” to users table .
ii.   Modify table dishes to enforce business rules “dishes.price must be
> 0.” using CHECK constraints
iii.   Select all veg, not-spicy dishes with price <= 250 from active
restaurants.
iv.   Show each restaurant’s average rating and number of ratings but
return only those with at least 2 ratings and an average ≥ 4.0.
v.   Create a view called user_ratings which will show each rating
alongside the user who gave it.
Q3   [20]
CO- 1 ;   a.   Convert the given ER diagram to tables   [10]
SO - 2;
BL- 2
--- page 3 ---
CO- 3 ;   b.   Consider the following relation used by a retail store to maintain sales   [10]
SO - 2;   information:
BL- 3
SALES(OrderID, OrderDate, CustID, CustName, CustCity,
ProdID, ProdName, UnitPrice, Quantity,
SalespersonID, SalespersonName, SalespersonRegion)
The following Functional Dependencies (FDs) are known to hold:
OrderID → OrderDate, CustID, SalespersonID
CustID → CustName, CustCity
ProdID → ProdName, UnitPrice
SalespersonID → SalespersonName, SalespersonRegion
{OrderID, ProdID} → Quantity
i.   Identify and explain partial and transitive dependencies present in
the given relation.   [3Marks]
ii.   Decompose the given relation step-by-step into Third Normal Form
(3NF).   [5 Marks]
iii.   Clearly show the final set of relations obtained after normalization,
with primary keys marked.   [2Marks]
Q4   [20]
CO- 1 ;   a.   Illustrate the major challenges encountered while creating a database from   [10]
SO - 2;   scratch with suitable examples
BL- 2
CO- 2 ;   b.   Solve the following queries using relational algebra   [10]
SO - 2;   Customer(CustomerID, CustomerName, City)
BL- 2   Order(OrderID, OrderDate, CustomerID)
Product(ProductID, ProductName, Price)
Order_Product(OrderID, ProductID, Quantity)
(i)   Find the names of customers who have placed at least one order.
(ii)   Get the customer names and order IDs for orders placed after
‘2024-01-01’.
(iii)   List all products ordered by customers who live in ‘Chicago’.
(iv)   List the products that have been ordered by any customer with a
quantity greater than 1
(v)   Find all customers located in New York and have ordered
something in month of October 2024(assume date is in yyyy-mm-dd)
Q5   [20]
CO- 2 ;   a.   Consider the following table:   [10]
SO - 2;   employee (employee_id, first_name , last_name, email, phone_number ,
BL-2   hire_date, job_id , salary, commission_pct , manager_id , department_id )
Write the SQL queries the following question:
i.   Write a SQL query to find those employees who receive a higher
salary than the employee with ID 163. Return first name, last name.
[3Marks]
--- page 4 ---
ii.   Write a SQL query to find out which employees have the same
designation as the employee whose ID is 169. Return first name,
last name, department ID and job ID. [3Marks]
iii.   Write a SQL query to find those employees whose salary matches
the lowest salary of any of the departments. Return first name, last
name and department ID [4Marks]
CO- 4 ;   b.   Problem Statement:   [10]
SO - 2;   Consider the following schedule S involving three transactions T1, T2, and T3
BL- 2   operating on data items A and B:
Step   T1   T2   T3
1   T1: Read(A)
2   T2: Read(A)
3   T1: Write(A)
4   T2: Write(A)
5   T3: Read(B)
6   T2: Read(B)
7   T2: Write(B)
8   T3: Write(B)
i.   Draw the precedence (serialization) graph for the given schedule S.
[2 Marks]
ii.   Determine whether the schedule is conflict-serializable or not. [3
Marks]
iii.   If it is, identify the equivalent serial or conflict equivalent schedule.
[3 Marks]
iv.   Briefly explain the importance of serializability in concurrent
transaction execution. [2Marks]
Q6   [20]
CO- 4 ;   a.   Define a transaction. Explain the ACID properties of a transaction with   [10]
SO - 2;   suitable examples. [5 Marks]
BL-3   Explain conflict serializability and view serializability with suitable
examples.
Also state how the two differ in terms of their conditions and scope.
[5Marks]
CO- 4 ;   b.   Compare and contrast ACID and BASE properties.   [10]
SO - 2;
BL-1
Q7   Write short notes on:   [20]
CO-1 ;   a   Advantages of DBMS   [5]
SO- 2;
BL-1
CO- 2 ;   b   Joins in SQL with examples of each.   [5]
SO - 2;
BL-2
--- page 5 ---
CO- 3;   c   Write a short note on Armstrong’s Axioms used for inferring functional   [5]
SO - 2;   dependencies.
BL-3   Explain the three basic rules with one example each and mention any
additional derived axioms that can be obtained from them.
CO- 4 ;   d   The difference between Key value based and Column Based storage type   [5]
SO - 2;   of NOSQL.
BL-1

‘2024-01-01’.
(iii)   List all products ordered by customers who live in ‘Chicago’.
(iv)   List the products that have been ordered by any customer with a
quantity greater than 1
(v)   Find all customers located in New York and have ordered
something in month of October 2024(assume date is in yyyy-mm-dd)

BL-2   hire_date, job_id , salary, commission_pct , manager_id , department_id )
Write the SQL queries the following question:
i.   Write a SQL query to find those employees who receive a higher
salary than the employee with ID 163. Return first name, last name.
[3Marks]

designation as the employee whose ID is 169. Return first name,
last name, department ID and job ID. [3Marks]

the lowest salary of any of the departments. Return first name, last
name and department ID [4Marks]

SO - 2;   Consider the following schedule S involving three transactions T1, T2, and T3
BL- 2   operating on data items A and B:
Step   T1   T2   T3
1   T1: Read(A)
2   T2: Read(A)
3   T1: Write(A)
4   T2: Write(A)
5   T3: Read(B)
6   T2: Read(B)
7   T2: Write(B)
8   T3: Write(B)
i.   Draw the precedence (serialization) graph for the given schedule S.
[2 Marks]

Marks]
iii.   If it is, identify the equivalent serial or conflict equivalent schedule.
[3 Marks]

--- page 4 ---

transaction execution. [2Marks]

SO - 2;
BL-1

In the airline reservation example, two users attempt to book the same
last available seat simultaneously. Isolation ensures that when one user's
booking transaction is being processed, the other user's transaction
cannot access or modify the same seat information until the first
transaction is completed.
This mechanism prevents conflicts and inconsistent results, ensuring that
only one user successfully books the seat.

Account A, but before the amount is credited to Account B, the server
crashes.
CO-4;   Case 2:   [10]
BL-2   Two users attempt to book the last available seat on a flight at the same
time using an airline reservation system. Both users submit their
booking requests simultaneously.
In the airline reservation example, two users attempt to book the same
last available seat simultaneously. Isolation ensures that when one user's
booking transaction is being processed, the other user's transaction
cannot access or modify the same seat information until the first
transaction is completed.
This mechanism prevents conflicts and inconsistent results, ensuring that
only one user successfully books the seat.

databases. Analyze the key differences in data modeling, scalability,

and consistency models. Justify scenarios where NoSQL would be

preferred over RDBMS with appropriate examples.

In the airline reservation example, two users attempt to book the same
last available seat simultaneously. Isolation ensures that when one user's
booking transaction is being processed, the other user's transaction
cannot access or modify the same seat information until the first
transaction is completed.
This mechanism prevents conflicts and inconsistent results, ensuring that
only one user successfully books the seat.

A hospital maintains data about its doctors, patients, and appointments.
Each doctor has a unique Doctor_ID, a name, specialization, and
consultation fee. Patients have a unique Patient_ID, name, age, address,
and contact number.
A patient can book multiple appointments with different doctors, and each
appointment has a unique Appointment_ID, along with date, time, and
status (confirmed, cancelled, completed).
********** 1 **********
--- page 2 ---
Each doctor may attend to multiple patients over time. The hospital also
keeps records of departments — each department has a Dept_ID and
Dept_Name, and every doctor belongs to exactly one department.

movie_cast (movie_id, actor_id, role_name, billing_order)
genres (genre_id, genre_name)
movie_genres (movie_id, genre_id)
Write SQL queries to perform the following operations
i.   List the English-language movies released after 2020 with runtime
> 140 minutes
ii.   For each director, show the number of movies they directed and the
average runtime of those movies. Include only directors with at
least 2 movies.
iii.   List movies whose title length ≥ 12 and whose uppercased title
starts with 'THE'.
iv.   Find actors who have acted in more than one movie.
v.   Find movies that belong to multiple genres (two or more).

Q1.The Prescriptions-R-X chain of pharmacies has offered to give you a free lifetime supply of medicines if
you design its database. Given the rising cost of health care, you agree. Here's the information that you gather:

e Patients are identified by an SSN, and their names, addresses, and ages must be recorded.

e Doctors are identified by an SSN. For each doctor, the name, specialty, and years of experience must
be recorded.

e Each pharmaceutical company is identified by name and has a phone number.

e For each drug, the trade name and formula must be recorded. «Eacn drug is sold by a given
pharmaceutical company, and the trade name identifies a drug uniquely from among the products of
that company. If a pharmaceutical company is deleted, you need not keep track of its products any
longer.

e Each pharmacy has a name, address, and phone number.

e Every patient has a primary physician. Every doctor has at least one patient.

e Each pharmacy sells several drugs and has a price for each. A drug could be sold at several pharmacies,
and the price could vary from one pharmacy to another.

* Doctors prescribe drugs for patients. A doctor could prescribe one or more drugs for several patients,
and a patient could obtain prescriptions from several doctors.

| e Each prescription has a date and a quantity associated with it. You can assume that if a doctor
prescribes the same drug for the same patient more than once, only the last such prescription needs to
be stored.

e Pharmaceutical companies have long-term contracts with pharmacies. A pharmaceutical company can
contract with several pharmacies, and a pharmacy can contract with several pharmaceutical
companies. For each contract, you have to store a start date, an end date, and the text of the contract.
--- page 2 ---
e Pharmacies appoint a supervisor for each contract. There must always be a supervisor for each
contract, but the contract supervisor can change over the lifetime of the contract.

Draw an ER diagram that captures the above information. (7 marks)

Convert the ER diagram to relational schema. (7 marks)

Q1.The Prescriptions-R-X chain of pharmacies has offered to give you a free lifetime supply
of medicines if you design its database. Given the rising cost of health care, you agree. Here's
the information that you gather:

e Patients are identified by an SSN, and their names, addresses, and ages must be
recorded. ;

e Doctors are identified by an SSN. For each doctor, the name, specialty, and years of
experience must be recorded.

e Each pharmaceutical company is identified by name and has a phone number.

e For each drug, the trade name and formula must be recorded. Each drug is sold by a
given pharmaceutical company, and the trade name identifies a drug uniquely from
among the products of that company. Ifa pharmaceutical company is deleted, you need
not keep track of its products any longer.

e Each pharmacy-has a name, address, and phone number.

e Every patient has a primary physician. Every doctor has at least one patient.

e Each pharmacy sells several drugs and has a price for each. A drug could be sold at
several pharmacies, and the price could vary from one pharmacy to another.

e Doctors prescribe drugs for patients. A doctor could prescribe one or more drugs for
several patients, and a patient could obtain prescriptions from several doctors.

e Each prescription has a date and a quantity associated with it. You can assume that if a
doctor prescribes the same drug for the same patient more than once, only the last such
prescription needs to be stored.

e Pharmaceutical companies have long-term contracts with pharmacies. A
pharmaceutical company can contract with several pharmacies, and a pharmacy can
contract with several pharmaceutical companies. For each contract, you have to store a
start date, an end date, and the text of the contract.

fs

e There are two types of employees in company- technical employee

e We store each EMPLOYEER’s social security number, address,
salary, sex, and birthdate.

e Each technical employee works for one department but may work on
several projects.

e We keep track of the number of hours per week that a technical
employee currently works on each project.

e The technical employee gains score based on work done. The latest
score of each technical employee is stored.

e The non-technical employees are given incentives based on their
work.

e Wealso keep track of the direct supervisor of each employee.

e Each employee may have a number of DEPENDENTs.

e For each dependent, we keep track of their name, sex, birthdate, and
relationship to the employee.

Ql. Ql. Computer Sciences Department frequent fliers have been complaining to Dane
County Airport officials about the poor organization at the airport. As a result, the officials
have decided that all information related to the airport should be organized using a DBMS, and
you've been hired to design the database. Your first task is to organize the information about
all the airplanes that are stationed and maintained at the airport. The relevant information is as
follows:

e Every airplane has a registration number, and each airplane is of a specific model.

e The airport accommodates a number of airplane models, and each model is identified
by a model number (e.g., DC-10) and has a capacity and a weight.

e A number of technicians work at the airport. You need to store the name, SSN,
address, phone number, and salary of each technician.

e ach technician is an expert on one or more plane model(s), and his or her expertise
may overlap with that of other technicians. This information about technicians must
also be recorded.

e Traffic controllers must have an annual medical examination. For each traffic
controller, you must store the date of the most recent exam.

e All airport employees (including technicians) belong to a union. You must store the
union membership number of each employee. You can assume that each employee is
uniquely identified by the social security number.

e The airport has a number of tests that are used periodically to ensure that air-planes
are still airworthy. Each test has a Federal Aviation Administration (FAA)
test number, a name, and a maximum possible score.

e The FAA requires the airport to keep track of each time that a given airplane is tested
by a given technician using a given test. For each testing event, the information
needed is the date, the number of hours the technician spent doing the test, and the
score that the airplane received on the test.
